# Squash

<strong>Main drivers:</strong>

Form, basis and where the squash was sampled; reporting limits decide how many results are non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/guerra2012-sao-paulo-vegetables-fruits-metals|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Guerra F, Trevizam AR, Muraoka T, Marcante NC, Canniatti-Brazaca SG · 2012</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/guerra2012-sao-paulo-vegetables-fruits-metals">Heavy metals in vegetables and potential risk for human health</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientia Agricola 69(1):54-60</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/guerra2012-sao-paulo-vegetables-fruits-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Norton GJ, Deacon CM, Mestrot A, Feldmann J, Jenkins P, Baskaran C, Meharg AA · 2015</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Cadmium and lead in vegetable and fruit produce selected from specific regional areas of the UK</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">The Science of the Total Environment 533, 520-527 (2015); Ask for lot results with a quantification limit well below the limit that applies in the destination market.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/guerra2012-sao-paulo-vegetables-fruits-metals|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Guerra F, Trevizam AR, Muraoka T, Marcante NC, Canniatti-Brazaca SG · 2012</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/guerra2012-sao-paulo-vegetables-fruits-metals">Heavy metals in vegetables and potential risk for human health</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Scientia Agricola 69(1):54-60</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/guerra2012-sao-paulo-vegetables-fruits-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Norton GJ, Deacon CM, Mestrot A, Feldmann J, Jenkins P, Baskaran C, Meharg AA · 2015</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/norton2015-britain-vegetables-fruit-cadmium-lead">Cadmium and lead in vegetable and fruit produce selected from specific regional areas of the UK</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">The Science of the Total Environment 533, 520-527 (2015); For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the squash came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.



<a id="ingredient-identity-and-scope"></a>
## What counts as squash

<a id="hmi-ing-001"></a>
<a id="what-commodity-biological-species-edible-part-and-commercial-forms-does-this-page-cover"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-002"></a>
<a id="which-processing-states-and-derivatives-are-distinct-materials-rather-than-synonyms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-003"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-can-be-transferred-between-those-materials-and-what-requires-a-separate-answer"></a>

This page covers squash in 2 forms kept separate in every comparison: squash, as sold (67 results) and squash, juice (1 result).<sup <div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-squash" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-squash-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of squash, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

data-selected-finding><strong class="hmi-finding">median &lt;5 µg/kg (below reporting limits)</strong><span class="hmi-row-note">median.</span><p>6 results. Basis: As sold, as eaten or fresh weight.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p></div><div class="hmi-selected-comparison" data-selected-comparison><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">HMI baseline position</p><p>Limited data · HMI squash Pb reference · Squash, as sold. median as reported: ≤5 µg/kg. HMI median 1–2 (bounded); Q1 ≤1.38; Q3 2–7 (bounded) µg/kg. 2 families. Reporting bounds prevent a rating.</p><p>Reporting bounds or insufficient reference spread prevent a stable Low / Moderate / High classification.</p><p>This finding’s sampling family contributes to the fixed reference.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sensitivity to weighting</p><p>Equal weight per subgroup: Limited data. The position remains unresolved under this alternative. This check does not address all sampling, rounding or analytical uncertainty.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sensitivity to source selection</p><ul><li>Without WHO GEMS/Food: Limited data.</li><li>Without U.S. Food and Drug Administration 2024: Limited data.</li></ul><p>These checks show dependence on each sampling family; they are not confidence intervals.</p><div data-baseline-reference="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:squash-6f9122a6c830:54970d9545ba"><a href="#hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:squash-6f9122a6c830:54970d9545ba">Reference, contributing evidence and methods</a></div></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Collection period</p><p data-timeline-scope>2000</p><div data-selected-timeline><p>No year-by-year sample register is reconstructed from this summary.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Geographic scope</p><p data-regional-scope>EURO</p><div data-selected-regional><p>Purchase or reporting market; growing area not identified.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sampling, analysis and reporting limits</p><p>As described by the monitoring programme.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p><p></p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Shared samples and study overlap</p><p>Subgroup rows from the same source share its laboratory and sampling frame; they are not independent studies.</p><p>Each table row describes a food/analyte result. ## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>

<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.



<a id="sampling-and-testing"></a>
## Testing squash for metals

<a id="hmi-ing-025"></a>
<a id="what-sampling-design-is-appropriate-for-the-ingredient-heterogeneity-lot-and-decision"></a>

<a id="what-can-a-certificate-of-analysis-or-non-detect-establish-and-what-remains-untested"></a>

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 0.667 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 3,000 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup References identify the sources used in these answers. The Sources index preserves additional discovery links; inclusion there does not mean a source has been reviewed for this assessment. Corrections, completed searches, new direct evidence and changes in requirements should reopen the relevant answer.


<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Squash FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-squash" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-squash" data-question="What heavy metals are found in squash?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in squash?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, dimethylarsinic acid, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn, Zn, V, Sr and Ti in squash, most often lead, cadmium and total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health <details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-squash-is-inorganic" data-anchor="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-squash-is-inorganic" data-question="How much of the arsenic in squash is inorganic?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How much of the arsenic in squash is inorganic?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Inorganic arsenic, the more toxic form, was measured in 2 results from 1 source, far fewer than total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported inorganic arsenic in squash, as sold from 19 results in 1 reporting region, with a subgroup median of 75.4 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
